Scientists have discovered that a known mushroom, Galerina indica, contains both psychedelic chemicals and deadly toxins.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A tiny brown mushroom found in Florida's swamps is the first known fungus to contain both psilocybin, the chemical compound in psychedelic mushrooms; and amatoxin, the poisonous substance in the world's deadliest mushrooms.

The species, Galerina indica, has been nicknamed the "dark magic mushroom" and was characterized in a study published Wednesday (Nov. 11) in the journal Current Biology.

"There is no species of mushroom anywhere, in any group, that has been known to produce both at the same time," study first author Andrew Kunik, a fungal biology researcher at the University of Florida, told Live Science. "So it's quite remarkable."

Exactly why the dark magic mushroom has evolved to contain these two compounds is unclear, but the authors told Live Science that they may give the fungus a powerful defense against being eaten.

The ability to produce either psychedelic or toxic chemicals has evolved multiple times across fungal lineages. A process called horizontal gene transfer, which is widespread among fungi, has played a part in that process.

In horizontal gene transfer an organism passes new genetic material to another, as opposed to vertical gene transfer, in which a parent passes genetic material to offspring. It can lead to unexpected genetic combinations and, in bacteria, plays a key role in the development of antibiotic resistance.

The tiny mushrooms seem to have acquired both psychedelic and toxic compounds via horizontal gene transfer.

Some of the world's deadliest mushrooms, from the Amanita genus, stop cells from producing proteins and can cause death through liver failure. Past work has shown these mushrooms shared the ability to produce their poisonous amatoxins via horizontal gene transfer with mushrooms from the unrelated genera Galerina and Lepiota. Some mushrooms have acquired the ability to produce psilocybin via horizontal gene transfer as well.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

But Kunik and colleagues weren't looking for specific chemicals when they began hunting for Galerina mushrooms in the swamps of Florida; they just wanted to learn more about the mushrooms' chemical makeup, as not all Florida Galerina mushrooms had been characterized before.

To find them both together in one mushroom, I never expected to find that.

They collected five specimens from Galerina, a genus that contains over 300 species worldwide, many of which are poisonous. Among the samples they collected were small G. indica specimens that grew on moss-covered palm trunks. The largest mushrooms were only about 0.2 inches (0.5 centimeters) tall, with caps up to about 0.1 inches (0.25 cm) wide.

Chemical analyses revealed that the dark magic mushroom contained both psilocybin and amatoxins, including one amatoxin that had never been documented in mushrooms. The new amatoxin wasn't wholly unexpected, given that other species of Galerina produce closely related amatoxins, Kunik said.

The production of psilocybin alongside the amatoxins was more of a surprise for the research team.

"To find them both together in one mushroom, I never expected to find that," study co-author Matthew E. Smith, a plant pathologist at the University of Florida, told Live Science. "We were shocked."

To understand how the tiny, deadly mushroom acquired its mind-altering, liver-poisoning abilities, the team compared the genes of G. indica with those of other psilocybin-producing mushrooms, finding the genes shared a common evolutionary origin — even though the mushrooms themselves are distantly related.

Scanning electron microscopy image of the spores from the “dark magic mushroom”.

The researchers also found G. indica’s psilocybin-producing genes are arranged in a different order than in other psilocybin-producing fungi, which suggests the gene cluster was rearranged during its evolutionary history, a clue that horizontal gene transfer happened.

G. indica seems to have acquired the ability to produce psilocybin via horizontal gene transfer from unrelated fungi about 15.5 million years ago, based on reconstructions of Galerina’s evolutionary history from DNA analysis, Smith said. However, it’s possible that psilocybin-producing genes entered the lineage through two separate horizontal gene transfer events, possibly as recently as 6 million years ago, Kunik noted. Collecting and sequencing more Galerina specimens could help untangle their evolutionary history.

The findings are surprising, said Hannah Reynolds, a fungal ecologist at Western Connecticut State University who was not involved with the new research. "What was exciting about this paper was seeing both of these [compounds] present, seeing that this gene cluster jumped in, got rearranged, maybe came in twice — it's very cool," Reynolds told Live Science.

The ability to produce two types of incapacitating chemicals could form a powerful defense against being eaten, because organisms that would normally eat them would have difficulty evolving defenses against both, Kunik speculated.

But the idea that this duo of compounds prevents the mushroom from being eaten is just a hypothesis. To buttress that idea, further research might tweak the genes of the dark magic mushroom to observe how removing one compound affects the species' role in the food web, Kunik suggested.

Tropical and subtropical mushrooms are historically understudied, so there is likely much more incredible fungal diversity right under our feet, Smith said.

Kunik et al. (2026). "The 'dark magic mushroom' co-produces amatoxins and psilocybin." Current Biology. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cub.2026.10.041

Education, not operations, are holding back reverse for purchase deals

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Reverse mortgage for purchase loans often close in seven to 25 days, panelists said at NRMLA Annual. Down payments typically run from 45% to 65%, and early counseling plus contract management can prevent delays.  AI Summary

Reverse mortgage for purchase loans work much like other types of home purchase financing. Even so, many real estate agents, financial advisers and loan officers don’t know how they work, according to experts who spoke this week at the National Reverse Mortgage Lenders Association (NRMLA)’s Annual Convention & Expo in San Antonio.

Peter Morris, an Arizona-based senior vice president at Revolution Mortgage, said the ideal reverse for purchase prospects are seniors who want to upsize, downsize or relocate. He also frequently works with borrowers who don’t qualify for traditional 30-year loans or home equity products due to debt-to-income or credit constraints.

Morris said he’s seeing rising interest in these financing options among real estate partners. He recently hosted a continuing education course where “I had the most Realtors that I’ve ever had in a CE class.”

Senior homeowners with high levels of equity and a need to move are able to utilize the federally insured Home Equity Conversion Mortgage (HECM) program or proprietary programs to purchase their next primary residence. The typical down payment ranges from 45% to 65%, depending on the appraised value, interest rate and age of the borrower. For example, an 80-year-old buyer has a smaller down payment requirement than a 70-year-old buyer since they have a shorter life expectancy.

Morris relayed a story about an agent partner in North Carolina with five listings between $400,000 and $800,000. While an all-cash buyer with $400,000 — typically a highly coveted buyer — could afford only some of these listings, a reverse for purchase borrower with the same $400,000 might be able to afford each of them, he told the agent.

“You have to educate your Realtor and show them that they are losing out, and they have the potential of making a lot more money with such a simple product,” Morris said. “I can show you how you can do less transactions and make more money, because you can buy more of a house in a reverse purchase.”

Shain Urwin, national reverse mortgage director for C2 Financial Corp., and Agi Glukhovskiy, SVP of business support and operations for Rate, explained some of the differences between traditional and reverse for purchase deals.

Borrower counseling through an independent third party is required on all HECM for Purchase deals insured by the Federal Housing Administration and for most proprietary reverse mortgages. Reverse transactions also have unique forms, including a Good Faith Estimate (GFE) and a HUD-1 form, rather than the Loan Estimate and Closing Disclosure forms commonly associated with forward mortgages.

Glukhovskiy said the absence of conventional forward lending documents are actually an advantage for reverse deals since they won’t have a mandatory federal waiting period. Urwin noted that a handful of states, such as California and Idaho, have implemented their own waiting periods. Beyond that, however, the steps are largely the same as any traditional deal as the process moves from application to underwriting, appraisal, title and approval.

The panelists sought to dispel ideas that reverse purchases are slow to close. Glukhovskiy said her team has wrapped them up in seven to 10 days, and although the average is 20 to 25 days, the idea that the process takes 45 days is “absolutely nonsense,” she added. Neil Sweren, a Baltimore-based reverse division manager for Atlantic Coast Mortgage, recalled a recent deal that closed in seven calendar days.

Angel Booth, national sales manager for title and settlement services provider Premier Reverse Closings, clarified to HousingWire‘s Reverse Mortgage Daily that the typical 10- to 15-day process cited by lenders is “absolutely attainable” once the loan is clear to close.

Glukovskiy said the keys to a quick closing are finishing counseling prior to the start of the home search process, collecting income documentation, and setting expectations with agents and lending operations staff. Loan officers should read the purchase contract and know every contingency date, Urwin said, as trouble begins when they overpromise. And Sweren pointed to timing: If a closing date gets pushed back and falls within six months of the borrower’s birthday, the amount of cash needed to close can change.

Simultaneous closings, where the borrower sells one home and buys another, can be difficult but not impossible to pull off. Glukovskiy recommended that sale proceeds should go straight to the title company handling the property being purchased, rather than going to the borrower.

Urwin told a cautionary tale about a borrower who took their sale proceeds and paid down a home equity line of credit on another property, leaving them ineligible to use the borrowed funds to buy their new house. “I learned from that very painfully, but those kinds of nuances matter when tracking those funds,” he said.

For loan officers who are new to reverse for purchase deals, panelists urged them to study up before they present the benefits to agents.

Continuing education courses are a prime way to teach agents, with Sweren saying they often come to his classes to help their own parents or grandparents. Many agents happen to be senior homeowners and may need assistance for themselves. Being the go-to loan officer when a listing includes a reverse mortgage should be part of everyone’s market strategy.

The panelists admitted that financial advisers may be a harder sell. Urwin noted that because these referral partners focus on cash and assets under management, they may not recommend a client put their cash into a home. But he relayed a success story where an adviser partner took out a reverse mortgage on his own home, then began sending referrals to Urwin when he had older clients who wanted to pay cash for a house.

The goal in these situations, Urwin said, should be to avoid an all-cash purchase. Clients should consider long-term tax strategies and the choice between leaving illiquid home equity or highly liquid cash to their heirs.

Sweren also mentioned the advantage of reverse mortgages in planning for rising healthcare costs, something he believes the industry “has not gotten past the tip of the iceberg” with. HECM lines of credit serve as “the most flexible borrowing instrument in the market by far,” he said, as borrowers can qualify for relatively large amounts, withdraw funds as needed, and have available proceeds grow over time based on the interest rate and annual mortgage insurance premium.

Sweren said reverse mortgage professionals should remember that “this is a very emotional transaction for seniors” as they may be leaving a home they’ve lived in for decades. They also may be widows or widowers who didn’t handle their family’s finances, meaning they’ll need additional support to get the deal over the line.

“Respecting that and understanding the emotional journey your client is on — and explaining that to the rest of the team that’s involved — is super helpful,” he added.

Alaska, Hawaiian Pilot MECs Approve Tentative Joint Contract

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Pilots at Alaska Airlines and Hawaiian Airlines are one step closer to a deal that will bring both work groups together under a single labor contract.

ALPA announced Wednesday that its Alaska and Hawaiian master executive councils unanimously approved a tentative agreement on a joint collective bargaining compact. The two councils had been negotiating since February 2025.

The proposed agreement will now go before the combined membership for a ratification vote.

Details of the agreement were not immediately available to the public.

Union officials said they worked to address the priorities of both work groups and have established a “contractual foundation for their future as a combined workforce.”

“This TA is the result of more than 600 days of challenging negotiations, countless hours of work by our volunteers, and an unwavering commitment to the pilots we represent,” Drew Coyle, the Alaska MEC chair, said in a statement. “Our Joint Negotiating Committee remained focused on the priorities of both pilot groups while working through some of the most complex issues facing our combined membership. Nothing about this process was easy. Now it’s our pilots’ turn to review the agreement, ask the tough questions, and decide whether it meets their expectations.”

Ratification voting starts Dec. 1 and closes Dec. 22.

Alaska and Hawaiian merged in 2024 and received a single operating certificate from the FAA in 2025. This effectively transformed Hawaiian from an independent airline to a brand under Alaska Air Group. Alaska has pledged to maintain Hawaiian’s brand identity, and Hawaiian retains a separate corporate leadership based in Honolulu.

The two brands have continued to integrate however, with Alaska now making use of long-haul aircraft originally owned by Hawaiian. Earlier this year, Alaska implemented a single passenger service system.

‘Pillaging Our Public Lands for AI Data Centers.’ Interior Lays Out New Plan to Sell Public Lands, Shrink Federal Workforce, and Ramp Up Development

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 11, 2026 3:44 PM EST

Get the hottest outdoor news from Outdoor Life.

The Department of the Interior released a five-year strategic plan Tuesday that outlines the federal government’s intentions to sell public lands, further reduce the workforce at its agencies, and continue to pursue natural resource development for energy dominance.

The 28-page document outlines four key goals for fiscal years 2026 through 2030. One of the most critical sections of the document for the conservation community is nested under the fourth goal of collaborating with states, tribes, and communities:

“The Department will consolidate its workforce to advance operational efficiencies,” reads the plan. “The Department will work to decrease the size of the federal estate by offering to states, localities and Tribes the opportunity to purchase federal lands. Through these sales, the Department will be able to focus its resources on primary federal responsibilities and nationally significant federal lands.”

According to the Interior, the proceeds from such sales of lands that aren’t considered “nationally significant” will go toward “purchasing entities to use for conservation purposes elsewhere” for “improved recreation access for American citizens, while at the same time decreasing the federal taxpayer’s financial responsibility to manage federal lands.”

“From a hunting and angling perspective, we love the places that are not of national significance,” Backcountry Hunters and Anglers president and CEO Ryan Callaghan tells Outdoor Life (we interviewed him while he was deer hunting a piece of no-name BLM ground out West). “Because they don’t have a big spotlight on them, and that’s where we go to hunt and fish.”

Selling and transferring federal lands — of any significance — is also wildly unpopular with American outdoorsmen and women.

“I’d need to see the criteria for disposal, but at first blush this flies in the face of overwhelming positive appreciation and sentiment for America’s public lands,” says Callaghan. “We’ve proved that middle-of-nowhere BLM land in Utah is of national significance during last summer’s budget reconciliation process. From an end-user standpoint, the American people want more public land and a workforce that can manage them responsibly.”

The staff cuts to the U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service, National Park Service, Bureau of Land Management (as well as the U.S. Forest Service, which is managed by the USDA) have also been a point of contention for hunters and anglers. As we reported in September, key public land agencies have already been chronically understaffed and underfunded for decades, with an accelerated decline under the second Trump administration.

Agency Staff CutsMeasured by direct civilian full-time equivalent

The only sectors where the DOI’s strategic plan promises to add staff are law enforcement and public safety — not conservation roles. That includes building a larger Park Police presence in Washington, D.C. The plan includes no staffing numbers or targets. These priorities are the heart of the objective: “Interior protects Americans and public lands.”

Transferring federal lands to states poses both the risk of further sell-off to private entities — and greater regulation for hunters, anglers, and outdoor users in general.

“People have got to understand [the restrictions of state land],” says Callaghan. “You can’t camp on state land in Colorado or Wyoming unless posted. You can’t drive on state land even on established roads unless they’re signed here in Montana. It’s illegal to shoot clay pigeons on state land in Montana. We just literally got de facto public access to our state lands in Montana about 20 years ago. State lands are private lands first, and the public access components come second to the ability to make money off those state lands. You’re not going to have [default state] public access unless it’s compatible in some way.”

The plan also merges wildland fire programs into the new U.S. Wildland Fire Service as part of a massive restructure of how the federal government fights wildfires.

Burgum also emphasizes the role of “super intelligence” throughout the DOI’s strategic plan.

“For the first time in human history, we can turn electricity into intelligence,” he writes, in the only bolded line in his letter that prefaces the plan. (Nowhere in the document does the DOI use the phrase “artificial intelligence.”)

The Trump administration is considering proposals for no fewer than a dozen data center projects on public land, as we reported in September. Together, those data centers could impact at least 17,600 acres of public land in the West.

“At every turn, the plan prioritizes extraction over conservation, emphasizing the need to pillage our public lands in order to power AI data centers,” Center for Western Priorities communications director Kate Groetzinger said in a statement Wednesday. “Of course, that’s no surprise given Burgum’s financial ties to the AI industry. But it flies in the face of the American public, who have made their feelings about data centers abundantly clear.”

Related: The Rush to Build Data Centers on Public Lands Is Worse Than You Thought

In the plan Burgum directs DOI employees to “harness technology to power efficiency,” pointing to efforts to replace manpower with artificial intelligence. The directive comes just days after the USFWS announced it wanted more “boots on the ground” to better manage our national wildlife refuges.

“Our team is actively expanding domestic energy production and securing our critical minerals supply chain — reducing reliance on foreign adversaries, accelerating innovation and protecting the global environment,” writes Burgum in the plan. “…Timber, grazing, geothermal energy, hydropower, outdoor recreation, hunting and fishing, mining, oil and gas and more contribute mightily to every facet of American life. As President Theodore Roosevelt, who helped shape and define our department, explained: ‘Conservation means development as much as it does protection.’”

Burgum is referencing a speech then-former President Roosevelt gave in 1910, in which he spoke forcefully against private interests. The full quote from Roosevelt reads:

“Conservation means development as much as it does protection. I recognize the right and duty of this generation to develop and use the natural resources of our land; but I do not recognize the right to waste them, or to rob, by wasteful use, the generations that come after us.”

The DOI’s strategic plan was released on the same day the BLM proposed a rule that would make the goals outlined in this plan easier to execute.

The potential rule is rooted in public-land rule making, but it would essentially overhaul the guidelines for writing BLM resource management plans. That includes eliminating the 90-day public comment period for major plans, throw out the currently required draft plan for public comment period, and potentially replace public comment periods with public meetings.

Related: Public Comment Periods Don’t Usually Matter Much to the Feds. Duck Hunters Made a Difference Anyway — for Now

“Those plans decide where drilling, mining, and grazing can happen on public lands, and which lands can be sold,” according to the Center for Western Priorities. “Under the Federal Land Policy Management Act, BLM can sell land only after a resource management plan identifies it for disposal.”

The proposed BLM rule is open for public comment through Jan. 11, 2027.

Roll-A-Rack debuting low-profile, manufactured-on-site solar racking

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Roll-A-Rack, a developer of patented, on-site-manufactured solar racking systems, will introduce SunChord, its new multi-purpose ground-mounted system, at RE+ 2026 in Las Vegas.

SunChord is a low-profile solar racking designed to use less land than conventional fixed-tilt racking or trackers. It is composed of two components: gutter channels and clamp brackets that attach to solar panels. SunChord assembles using what Roll-A-Rack has dubbed its “snap-slide-secure” system.

“The next generation of solar technology needs to do more than produce clean electricity. It needs to make better use of land, minimize environmental impacts and create value beyond the energy generated,” said Don Scipione, founder of Roll-A-Rack. “SunChord reflects our vision of harmonizing sun and earth, combining solar energy production with rainwater harvesting and soil conservation in a system that can unlock new opportunities for solar development on land where conventional approaches fall short.”

Roll-A-Rack claims an array using SunChord can capture 95% of rain that falls it using its racking channels to redirect the water. The company sees potential for the racking in agrivoltaic applications, sites with land constraints, on brownfields and where ground disturbance should be limited.

SunChord is assembled on-site using a roll-forming device similar to producing roof-edge gutters. SunChord was developed with support from the U.S. Department of Energy’s Integrated Energy Systems Office and the USDA National Institute of Food and Agriculture. The company has two issued U.S. patents, a patent application pending, wind-tunnel ratings and UL 2703 certification.

Roll-A-Rack is planning to supply SunChord for up to five pilot projects. Interested EPCs and developers can contact the company at [email protected].

United Airlines Finally Plans Boeing 757-300 Makeover With Seatback Screens And Bigger Bins

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

United Airlines plans to give its Boeing 757-300 fleet a long-overdue cabin update, adding larger overhead bins, entertainment screens at every seat, and power throughout the cabin. These aircraft have needed attention for years and were always included in the United Next refresh plan, though I am somewhat surprised to see United keep these workhorses in the fleet.

United Airlines 757-300 aircraft will be modified in accordance with the United’s “United Next” strategy, which includes installation of new Boeing Space Bins, Panasonic Audio Video On Demand (AVOD), In Flight Entertainment at every seat location, full cabin In-Seat Power Supply System (ISPS), new class dividers and cabin headers, and new UAL branding elements. Additionally, all seats and monuments will be refreshed throughout the cabin.

United:“United Airlines 757-300 aircraft will be modified in accordance with the United's "United Next" strategy, which includes installation of new Boeing Space Bins, Panasonic Audio Video On Demand (AVOD), In Flight Entertainment at every seat location, full cabin In-Seat…

As I mentioned above, the surprise is not that United is refreshing its entire narrwobody fleet, which it has promised for years now, but that it is actually moving forward with the 757-300s rather than phasing them out. However, I also understand that an A321neo or 737 MAX is not necessarily a direct replacement for this workhorse.

My understanding is that one reason this project took longer to get moving was the lack of a certified larger-bin option for this particular aircraft. United had to work through that certification hurdle rather than simply order an existing installation, which puts the delay in context.

United listed just 21 Boeing 757-300s in its year-end 2025 fleet disclosure, each with 234 seats. Undertaking a specialized modification for a fleet that small is a more substantial commitment than fitting the same equipment across hundreds of aircraft. For passengers, however, the size of the subfleet is beside the point. If your flight is operated by a 757-300, the overhead storage and cabin condition are part of your United experience.

More useful bin space should make boarding less stressful: currently there are far too many bags that must be gate checked because the smaller overhead bins force larger (but standard) carry-on bags to be placed on their sides.

I will also be happy to see entertainment screens included throughout the aircraft. When traveling with my children, individual screens are especially useful.

JonNYC also mentions power and it will be great to have USB-C, but the aircraft already have universal A/C power outlets in every cabin.

But what JonNYC does not mention and I consider most important is that these aircraft will get Starlink. Currently, most 757-300s still have an ancient Panasonic Wi-Fi system that often does not work. I specifically avoid the 757-300 for this reason and have been burned almost every time I have flown it with non-operable Wi-Fi. Some 757-300s have been upgraded with Viasat, but I am unwilling to fly on the 753s unless absolutely necessary. The new cabins will be welcomed, but Starlink is the most important upgrade.

I do not object to an aircraft simply because it is older: Delta has shown that a 30-year-old aircraft with mood lighting, fast Wi-Fi, and seatback screens can feel just as comfortable as a more modern aircraft. Refreshed seating and better amenities can change that experience substantially, even when the aircraft itself has been flying for decades.

This investment suggests United sees a continuing role for the 757-300 for many years in the future.

The Boeing 757-300 is finally in line for the kind of cabin attention it has long needed. Larger bins, seatback entertainment, and Starlink Wi-Fi will make a noticeable difference. I figured these aircraft would just be retired, but it appears they will fly the “Friendly Skies” for many years to come.

Pompliano Says Bitcoin Spot ETFs Are Safer and More Convenient Than Hardware Wallets for Bitcoiners Holding Over $10,000

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Pompliano’s comments on Bitcoin hardware wallets and Bitcoin ETFs come at a time when more people show interest in BTC amid security threats.

Bitcoin expert Anthony Pompliano today tackled a crucial question that serious Bitcoin investors often ask. The question is: between hardware wallets and ETFs, which one offers the best outcome for securing and getting exposure to Bitcoin?

In an interview released early today, the celebrated crypto analyst answered questions savvy investors want answered about Bitcoin self-custody versus Bitcoin ETFs.

Today, Bitcoin adoption grows at a remarkable rate. As more investors seek to invest in the cryptocurrency, there are two major ways to do so: gaining exposure through Bitcoin hardware wallets (self-custody) or Bitcoin ETFs.

In the podcast, Pompliano admitted that using spot ETFs is more secure than storing Bitcoin in a hardware wallet. According to the crypto expert, spot ETFs offer an accessible approach, providing investors with a simplified, regulated way to buy and invest in Bitcoin through their existing brokerage accounts. As Pompliano stated, ETFs stand out because they emphasize the importance of regulation in providing investor protection and market integrity.

Pompliano further recognized spot ETFs as the safest and the most convenient option for investors holding between $10,000 and $10 million worth of BTC. He said these investment vehicles offer greater liquidity than directly holding BTC on hardware wallets. They provide investors with diversified exposure to stock exchanges and on-chain markets.

However, he said convenience comes with caveats, as ETFs provide indirect exposure to Bitcoin. This means that investors don’t actually own the underlying Bitcoin. Also, ETFs might not provide investors with the same level of control as directly owning BTC in self-custody wallets.

This highlights the advantages of Bitcoin hardware wallets, which allow customers to hold BTC directly, giving them greater control. However, this level of control comes with the responsibility of managing private keys, asset control, and security risks.

The debate between self-custody and ETFs comes at a time when the crypto industry continues to experience increasing security breaches, with the latest and largest one being the Binance infiltration.

Such unfortunate hacking events compel customers to rethink how to secure their BTC while still accessing strong liquidity. The self-custody approach offers direct ownership and control of BTC, though it requires technical know-how and involves greater security risks. On the other hand, ETFs offer a simple, regulated way to gain exposure to the asset, with diversified liquidity. They, however, come with some limitations.

Report: Giants called Falcons about a potential Bijan Robinson trade

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

The Giants didn’t make any big deals at the trade deadline, but they tried to make a very big one.

The Giants called the Falcons to see whether running back Bijan Robinson was available in a trade, according to Jordan Raanan of ESPN.

That would have been a blockbuster, but it’s unsurprising that the teams didn’t make a deal. The Falcons made a big investment in Robinson this offseason, signing him to a three-year, $66.8 million contract extension, and from all accounts they view him as one of the most important pieces of their franchise.

There’s no word on what the Giants were willing to offer, or whether the Falcons engaged at all, or simply told the Giants, “Not interested.”

The Giants did make one big trade this season, acquiring J.J. McCarthy, who has played well enough for the Giants to win their first two starts and remain in first place in the NFC East. Acquiring Robinson would have been a strong sign that the Giants believe they’re serious contenders, this year. That the Giants even tried showed that coach John Harbaugh meant it when he said they’re a team trying to win now, not build for the future.

How Michael Carrick's Man United fell apart: Players' bust-up on flight back from Madrid, weakness in training and on touchline - and why dressing-room sources believe divided board 'hung him out to dry'

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

By CHRIS WHEELER, NORTHERN SPORTS WRITER and NATHAN SALT - FOOTBALL REPORTER

Published: 10:30 EST, 11 November 2026 | Updated: 06:44 EST, 12 November 2026

For the last two days, Michael Carrick has followed his familiar route towards Manchester United’s training base, nudging his black Land Rover past the security barrier at Carrington before 8am, and making his way into work just as he has for much of the last two decades as a player, coach and head coach at the club.

While Carrick's fate has been decided by men in suits in Monaco and Manchester, he has put on his training gear and gone through the motions of coaching whichever players are left during the international break.

It has become increasingly apparent that Carrick was a dead man walking in the two weeks since the United board gave him a stay of execution and bought themselves time after the 3-0 defeat by Bournemouth last month.

Having effectively been brought in as a caretaker when United plucked him off the beach in Barbados in mid-January, he left much the same way: merely keeping the seat warm for Mauricio Pochettino in the chaotic days that have led to his sacking.

Sources close to the United dressing room believe Carrick has been ‘hung out to dry’ as United dithered over his future and then began a futile attempt to appoint Pochettino as his replacement in secret.

This is the story of how Carrick's United fell apart. It is the story of how disgruntled players aired their dirty laundry in public and clashed in private. It is the story of how the club's boardroom became the backdrop for an executive power struggle. And it is the story of how the magic touch that saw Carrick land the job permanently just five months ago deserted him...

Michael Carrick is out as Manchester United manager - just ten league games after guiding United to third and being handed a two-year contract

Carrick has had to turn up as normal at Carrington this week even as the world knew he was for the chop. Some dressing-room sources say he has been 'hung out to dry'

Carrick would have seen the writing on the wall when he got up to give an impressive speech at a dinner in the Heritage Suite at Old Trafford on Friday night to celebrate the 40th anniversary of Sir Alex Ferguson’s appointment.

The significance of the occasion was not lost on Carrick. Ferguson was brought in to succeed Ron Atkinson on the same date in November 1986 with United one place above the relegation zone. Having boxed himself into an identical corner, Carrick too was on borrowed time.

Two days later, he stood helplessly on the touchline at Old Trafford as United played out a ridiculous 4-4 draw with Aston Villa. Twice his team fought back from two goals down, but it was the final proof for United bosses that change was unavoidable.

The biggest club in English football having to endure the humiliation of at least two weeks in the relegation zone was too much for them to stomach.

His body language after the Bournemouth game when a tearful and crestfallen Carrick offered what looked like a farewell wave to the Stretford End – four days after he approached fans in Como to apologise for an equally painful 4-1 Champions League surrender – suggested he was already done.

The 45-year-old limped on to another defeat at Chelsea, then presided over a barmy 3-2 comeback win over Roma and the bonkers draw with Villa. A consummate and composed midfielder for United, Carrick of all people knew this wasn’t the way football should be played.

All the while, he spoke like a man who could turn things around. They always do. But the performances and results told a different story.

His critics would argue that someone whose only previous job in management was at Middlesbrough should never have been put in charge of United in the first place.

Others would say the bravest move by the club would have been to thank Carrick for his admirable efforts in securing third place and Champions League qualification last season, and move on in the summer when Pochettino would have been available for free before signing a new contract as US coach with a £7million release clause.

Matheus Cunha's late winner sealed a remarkable comeback win over Roma last week - but it promised to be a false dawn for Carrick

The 45-year-old's body language after an earlier loss to Bournemouth had suggested he was done back then, with Carrick appearing to wave farewell to the Stretford End

Instead, Carrick was rewarded with a two-year deal in May and the wheels fell off in spectacular fashion. Rather like his friend and former team-mate Ole Gunnar Solskjaer, the honeymoon period gave way to something altogether less appealing.

At least Solskjaer stuck around for three more years. Having beaten the Norwegian to the job in January, Carrick’s reign lasted less than 10 months.

The similarities between the two men are striking. Ex-players who basked in the glory days under Ferguson and know what it takes to succeed at Old Trafford, but were incapable of lifting the modern-day Manchester United to similar heights.

Having replaced Ruben Amorim and inspired a remarkable turnaround in the second half of last season, Carrick must have thought he had a chance.

His calm demeanour and experience of life in the Old Trafford pressure cooker that had played a part in his appointment was in contrast to the emotional incontinence of Amorim. How strange then that it has given way to such calamity and chaos.

United got off to the worst possible start by losing at newly-promoted Hull City in August. Tigers boss Sergej Jakirovic belittled them by saying his goalkeeper Konstantinos Tzolakis might as well have been sipping a cup of tea, and the hits have just kept on coming for Carrick.

Aside from Bournemouth, there were the late equalisers scored by Ainsley Maitland-Niles and Dominic Solanke for Everton and Tottenham, the controversial winner conceded to 10-man Manchester City, crashing out of the Carabao Cup to Brighton after giving up a two-goal lead, and the defeats in Europe by Atletico Madrid and Como after going behind in the opening seconds.

By the time Alysson scored in the first minute for Villa on Sunday, United’s defence had become as leaky as the Old Trafford roof. The four goals Carrick’s side conceded took it to 15 in his final four games.

United's defence has been leaky all season long, with Ainsley Maitland-Niles' stoppage-time equaliser at Hill Dickinson Stadium among the goals to cost them valuable points

By the time Alysson scored in the first minute for Aston Villa on Sunday, United’s defence had become as leaky as the Old Trafford roof. They let in 15 in his final four games

So why the alarming decline from last season? The United players haven’t looked sharp after a disjointed World Cup summer that involved a more relaxed one-week training camp in Ireland. There have been too many injuries.

Some players have spoken of a drop-off in training under Carrick and his backroom staff. Others feel that the switch to a more possession-based 4-2-4 system was asking too much of a group who aren’t suited to it.

There was surprise that a man so invested in the club's academy used so few young players. Harry Amass was left kicking his heels despite the chronic shortage of left backs, JJ Gabriel's shock exit was an unwelcome distraction, and Shea Lacey's re-emergence came too late.

It would be grossly unfair to pin this all on Carrick though. He is just the latest unfortunate to pay the price for the shambles at United which runs from the boardroom to the dressing room.

Amid ongoing uncertainty over his future, there has been a power struggle behind the scenes between director of football Jason Wilcox and director of recruitment Christopher Vivell over who to replace him, while sources close to the Glazer family have revealed a growing dissatisfaction at how minority owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe and Ineos are running the club.

United failed Carrick in the transfer market last summer with a futile pursuit of his No 1 midfield target Elliot Anderson, and instead delivered Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos.

Carrick could see trouble on the horizon when he spoke to the media at Carton House in Maynooth during that pre-season camp in early August, but tried not to rock the boat.

It was the day his daughter Louise was getting her A-level results. ‘You know, we would like more, we need to keep pushing for more,’ he said, using the word ‘push’ another nine times.

There has been a power struggle behind the scenes between director of football Jason Wilcox (centre) and director of recruitment Christopher Vivell (left) over who to replace Carrick

Some players have spoken of a drop-off in training under Carrick and his backroom staff

Carlos Baleba followed but an ankle injury meant that he couldn’t play until October, and the £70m signing then suffered a hamstring injury.

United blundered catastrophically by focusing on midfield and delaying improvements to other areas of the team until next summer. In particular, the decision not to sign cover for Luke Shaw and Benjamin Sesko backfired spectacularly as both players succumbed to injury.

Lisandro Martinez called out United’s underwhelming recruitment in Madrid by saying he expected more of Wilcox, and he isn’t the only one who feels that way.

Tielemans gave a terse ‘no comment’ when he was asked on camera about his team-mate’s remarks, but it’s understood the two players clashed on the team flight back to Manchester as cracks began to show in an increasingly unhappy camp.

The camaraderie fostered by rounds of golf involving Carrick and the players during the break in Ireland was beginning to fall apart.

It came to a head when captain Bruno Fernandes became the second member of the leadership group to speak out of turn, questioning Carrick’s tactics after the loss to Como.

Having continuously defended his players in the face of criticism from some of his former United team-mates over their lack of effort, Carrick’s spiky response that ‘if you don't run forward and you don't run back, you aren’t going to win’ perhaps revealed his true feelings about this group.

He and Fernandes settled their differences in a clear-the-air meeting, but the questions didn’t go away. When he was asked about their relationship again at the end of what proved to be his final pre-match press conference last Friday, Carrick tapped his inquisitor on the shoulder as he left the room at Carrington and said with a smile: ‘Nice try!’

It was typical of Carrick, who often met questions with the knowing grin of a man who couldn’t say what he really thought.

Lisandro Martinez and Youri Tielemans clashed on United's flight back to Manchester following the former's incendiary comments after a loss to Atletico Madrid

One issue has been Carrick’s rather passionless appearance on the touchline. ‘He doesn't upset anyone, but doesn't inspire anyone either,’ remarked one dressing room source

It got to the point where he was encouraged by the club to be a little more forthcoming in press conferences. There have been some barbed post-match exchanges in TV interviews with Sky Sports and Viaplay this season, and the five-minute monologue before the Bournemouth game was pure theatre.

But on the whole, Carrick’s ability to play a straight bat in front of the cameras was a welcome change for the Old Trafford hierarchy compared to the loose-lipped Amorim – particularly with the Amazon Prime documentary-makers following their every move.

‘He talks a lot but doesn't say much,’ said Roy Keane, never Carrick’s biggest fan, on Sunday. ‘I don't know if that's a tactic, like a politician.’

More of an issue was Carrick’s rather passionless appearance on the touchline, which seemed to be reflected in his team at times.

Always dressed in black, a hand placed studiously on his chin, it has contrasted with more animated adversaries such as Unai Emery, Roberto De Zerbi and Diego Simeone in recent weeks.

‘He doesn't upset anyone, but doesn't inspire anyone either,’ remarked one dressing room source.

The fans were always behind him though, even the ones who may have accepted some time ago that a change was needed. Support and sympathy were never in short supply for a coach who has been let down by the men in the directors’ box and the players on the pitch.

That much has been abundantly clear by the abuse meted out to Ratcliffe and Co this season, and the unprecedented scenes when the fans turned on the players after Bournemouth.

Carrick looks set to be replaced by former Tottenham and Chelsea boss Mauricio Pochettino (right)

Carrick has been known to stand alongside them in the past, and it would be no surprise to see him there again in future.

‘Oh, it’s Carrick you know…’ echoed his tribute song from the Stretford End as he disappeared down the tunnel for the last time on Sunday evening.

So another bites the dust. A good man. A United man. The right man for the job? Maybe not, as it turns out. That poisoned chalice now passes to Mauricio Pochettino.

This tiny box gives your older tech smart home superpowers – and it’s only $69

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Home Assistant gets new equipment to battle planned obsolescence and abandoned cloud services.

Home Assistant is perhaps the most versatile smart home automation system of them all, thanks to the extreme customization power it offers. Today, a tiny, new box-shaped device is giving Home Assistant users a way to make their existing electronics smarter, rather than replacing them to keep up with the times.

Also: Ring’s new drone patrols your home to keep an eye on things

The Home Assistant Connect AUX-2 is a multi-purpose piece of hardware made by Nabu Casa, the company behind official Home Assistant hardware. The device brings together several connectivity options, including RS-232 serial for older TVs and other equipment, and the ability to connect older stereo systems and amplifiers to modern multi-room audio setups.

“The most sustainable device is the one you already own,” said Paulus Schoutsen, founder of Home Assistant and Nabu Casa. “No device should be left behind because it doesn’t have smart capabilities, or worst of all, its cloud services get turned off.

With the AUX-2, Home Assistant is joining the fight against planned obsolescence and abandoned cloud services.

Modern smart home systems have a major drawback: they exclude working electronics simply because they weren’t designed to integrate with today’s smart home platforms. This is the problem that the AUX-2 addresses.

Instead of buying a new stereo system with smart features, consumers can use the $69 AUX-2 to make the devices they already own smarter, more connected, and easier to control.

A single AUX-2 can control multiple devices, depending on how they’re connected and the interfaces they use. The device’s limit has more to do with the number of physical connections required.

Also: How I open-sourced my smart home – and ditched Google for Siri

The AUX-2 lets you connect two serial-controlled devices directly through the RS-232 ports. The USB port can act as an expansion for compatible adapters. You can place one AUX-2 on an entertainment center and connect an older stereo and TV, then use another AUX-2 elsewhere in your house for additional equipment with physical serial connections.

According to Home Assistant, with an AUX-2, you can control an old AV receiver without the original app, have an older TV respond to commands without relying on its manufacturer’s cloud servers, or integrate existing audio equipment into modern multiroom sound systems.

This way, consumers won’t need to trash a functional piece of equipment simply because the manufacturer stops supporting it.

Aside from RS-232 serial, the AUX-2 also supports Bluetooth, Z-Wave, USB, PoE, and Wi-Fi for connectivity.

Reinstated Aurora Gaming beat F9 EICAR to win Valorant Europe Qualifier 1

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Aurora Gaming came from a map down to beat F9 EICAR 2-1 and win the first VCT EMEA Kickoff Europe open qualifier. The win came days after they were put back into the bracket when the UK team BONK was disqualified for cheating.

The VCT EMEA Kickoff: Europe Qualifier 2027 decider got under way at 18:45. The series began on Lotus. Aurora’s chiwa was named most valuable player of the series.

The Aurora roster lists Boo, chiwa, Derke, B1SK and trexx. F9 EICAR, based in France, fielded Kipperman, Destrian, Tenshi, izana and Geekkooo.

Aurora’s run nearly ended early. BONK, a UK Valorant roster, beat them 2-1 in the round of 32 on 6 November. That result followed a comeback from 3-11 down on Bind. Riot’s ruling against BONK reversed Aurora’s exit. Every BONK player and registered team member was provisionally suspended. Project 100K and ycatble returned to the bracket along with Aurora.

The qualifier left no room for error. It was a single-elimination bracket for 256 teams, and every match was a best-of-three. Once reinstated, Aurora did not drop a map before the final. They beat Pixel Lumina 2-0 in the second semi-final.

In the first semi-final, xXxMasterProMegaTeamxXx fell 2-1 to F9 EICAR. The French side had arrived at the qualifier in mixed form. F9 EICAR lost 0-2 to WIP Esports in VCL France: Revolution Stage 2 2026 and were twice beaten 1-2 by Mandatory. A 2-1 result against Esprit Shōnen was their only victory in that run.

The Elo power rankings show the gap on paper. F9 EICAR sit at rank 241, with a rating of 1405 and a 6-16 record in 22 rated matches. Aurora are unranked.

The final still carried weight. Seeding for the playoff stage would be decided by the result, Insider Gaming reported.

The stakes are clear. Under Riot’s 2027 system, four non-partner teams will reach VCT EMEA Kickoff. Two will come from Europe, one from Türkiye and one from the Middle East and North Africa.

— Aurora Valorant (@Aurora_Valorant) November 10, 2026

That leaves European hopefuls chasing places at a 12-team LAN event. VCT 2027 EMEA Kickoff will be run by Riot Games at the Riot Games Arena in Berlin.

The playoff field is not yet complete. The top four teams from each open qualifier move on, and the second Europe open qualifier is set for 12 to 15 November. The playoffs will use a modified double-elimination bracket of best-of-three matches, with a best-of-five grand final. The top two finishers earn places at VCT 2027 EMEA Kickoff, whose window runs from 14 January to 7 February.

Forget Salmon: This Fast-Cooking Fish Has Fewer Calories

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Salmon's rich intensity also means it generally has more calories, which can mean different things based on someone's dietary needs. If you're conscious of this and still want something that cooks up quickly (and deliciously), try flounder, a leaner, lower-calorie option that never lacks flavor.

Exact calorie amounts can vary quite a bit, especially when it comes to wild versus farmed salmon, but even the leanest ones usually sit between 130 and 200 calories per three-ounce serving. Flounder populations, however, have a more uniform lifestyle and consistently clock in at around 100 calories per three-ounce serving. That brown or gray layer of fat may help a salmon migrate and survive cold waters, but flounder are sedentary ambush predators that don't have nearly the same energy requirements. On your plate, this means a fillet of flounder is lean, clean-tasting, and quite mild compared to a richly marbled piece of salmon.

Because flounder is often thinner and far less fatty than salmon, that also means it cooks exponentially quicker. While a skin-on fillet can widen your margin of error, it's still much easier to render your tender fish into a dense, rubbery mess. Your best bet is to treat it like a lean steak and use a hot and fast cooking method, letting a bit of carryover cooking finish the center rather than trying to reach the perfect temperature in a pan or on the grill.

Flounder is delicate, light, and can easily lose its natural flavor if seasoned too heavily. While it's not exactly a beginner-friendly fish, the best ways to cook it are often straightforward and focus on timing and temperature, not technique. Hands-off methods that require little manipulation or rely on a sauce for flavor are usually ideal and keep your meat moist and tender.

If you haven't tried making a meunière yet, it's a classic seafood dish you should try at least once. Made by combining browned butter, lemon, and parsley, it's just flavorful enough to elevate your flounder without overwhelming it. Just about any French butter sauce, from beurre blanc to béchamel, is great with flounder because it adds a bit of richness and is a great vehicle for other tasty seasonings. Plus, they're flavorful enough that you'll only have to pan-sear each side of your fish, reducing the chance that it'll fall apart or overcook.

Flounder grows more delicate the more it's cooked, so preparation methods that rely on you only handling it when raw are also quite ideal. Baking is a precise method, and you only have to move the fish once to transfer it to a plate. On the other hand, flounder is fantastic when deep-fried, and the breading helps it hold its shape. You'll still want to use a large, slotted tool and a delicate hand when fishing it out, but all you need to do is batter and drop it into oil for a great result.

Visibility Gets You On The List, Evidence Helps You Stay There via @sejournal, @billhunt

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

AI visibility can hide evidence gaps that weaken buyer recommendations as requirements get specific, making decision readiness a crucial GEO metric.

For much of the past two years, the AI search conversation has focused on visibility. Teams want to know whether their brand is mentioned, where it appears in recommendations, how often competitors surface, and whether they are being cited across ChatGPT, Gemini, Claude, Perplexity, Google, and other AI surfaces.

Those are useful metrics, but I have increasingly questioned whether they tell us enough about the corresponding business outcome.

Nearly two years ago, I began using the term “eligibility gates” to describe the detailed decision criteria that determine whether a company or product remains qualified as a buyer moves through the decision process. I later expanded on that idea when I wrote Decision Coverage: whether an organization has exposed enough information and evidence for AI to evaluate, compare, qualify, and confidently recommend.

That led to two related arguments tied to previous articles. In my article Beyond Content Parity, I argued that using tools and processes that simply match competitor content may create parity without creating any decision advantage. In Beyond Brand Sovereignty, I argued that the knowledge AI needs often exists beyond the marketing site, scattered across product, sales, support, engineering, legal, customer success, and external sources.

A recent project allowed me to test those ideas against an actual business problem.

The company did not have an AI visibility problem. It frequently appeared in AI-generated consideration sets. The concern was what happened afterward, when prospective buyers continued the conversation and began asking more specific questions about requirements, workflows, integrations, outcomes, evidence, and competitive differences.

The question was not “How do we get seen in AI?” It was, “Why, with all this visibility, is our lead pipeline shrinking?” The reality we found was that as AI took a prospective buyer further down the consideration set, they became less competitive.

To prove my thesis and identify the problem, we created 38 detailed, criteria-based prompts across several product categories and buyer segments, and evaluated them on five AI surfaces, yielding 190 responses.

These were intentionally different from broad “best vendor” prompts. They reflected the kinds of requirements buyers use to narrow a field: workflow fit, integrations, regulatory requirements, interoperability, operational conditions, analytics, platform breadth, and other decision criteria. I had to create a detailed explanation of their purpose as diagnostic-only because the brand and their SEO agency argued that no human would ever use that complex a prompt. We actually found that some prompts were even longer after talking to actual procurement managers.

The initial numbers looked excellent – even better than their current visibility scorecard. Our research found the company was mentioned in 98.4% of evaluated responses and appeared as either a primary recommendation or shortlisted option 93.2% of the time. For many GEO programs, that would be the headline of the week and champagne for everyone. The brand was almost always visible and almost always treated as a serious option. But visibility was not the problem we were investigating.

When we evaluated those same responses against the decision criteria in the prompts, 73.7% had weak or missing evidence supporting important claims or recommendations about the company. In 15.8% of runs, an important association, decision criterion, competitive distinction, or discovery signal contained what we classified as a Significant or Critical Gap. The more we refined our evidence-scoring criteria, the higher this number rose.

The critical finding was that AI systems generally knew the company belonged in the conversation. However, they were far less consistent at substantiating why it should remain there once the buyer began applying specific eligibility criteria. That is the distinction that matters and explains their declining lead pipeline.

There is a meaningful difference between asking which vendors operate in a market and asking which vendors can satisfy a specific combination of technical, operational, regulatory, and business requirements.

A model may know a company is a legitimate vendor, understand its main products, and have enough general information to put it on a shortlist. But once the buyer asks how an integration works, whether a workflow is supported, what happens under a particular operating condition, what limitations apply, or what evidence supports an outcome claim, the information requirements become more demanding. Those are not merely additional keywords. They are evidence requirements.

That explains how a brand can achieve a 98.4% mention rate and 93.2% primary-or-shortlist rate while still having weak or missing evidence in nearly three-quarters of the responses. The numbers are not contradictory. They measure different stages of the decision process.

No one can dispute that AI visibility gets the company into the consideration set. Decision readiness determines whether AI has enough information to keep it there.

See also: B2B Buyers Choose A Vendor Before They Reach Out – 3 Ways To Be Visible When It Counts

One of the more important findings was that weak evidence did not necessarily mean the company lacked the capability. In many cases, the capability appeared to exist, but the available information did not explain or substantiate it well enough to satisfy the AI surface’s confidence level.

A marketing bullet might claim an end-to-end process without ever showing the actual process, connections, or how the data flows between systems. We found efficiency claims but did not have any “proof” from internal tests or customer testimonials of the efficiency gain.

Sometimes the information was simply fragmented. A model might need to connect a product page to an integration page, then to another interoperability page, before it could infer how the pieces satisfied the buyer’s requirement. While an AI system could or should be able to make those multi-part connections, each additional inference creates another opportunity for incomplete representation or for a competitor with clearer evidence to become the safer recommendation.

We also found that the strongest content was not necessarily the longest or most buzzword-integrated, but rather content that made the relationships among capability, workflow, buyer requirements, and outcome explicit.

An interesting finding was that only first-party evidence was not inherently a weakness. Many claims lacked third-party validation, but this was not necessarily flagged as a defect. We found that authoritative product documentation may be the best evidence that a feature or integration exists. However, every claim involving superiority, effectiveness, adoption, scale, measurable outcomes, or competitive differentiation did benefit from independent corroboration by customers, industry, government, analysts, or other parties.

That means some evidence gaps are not content-writing problems at all, but does anyone else agree with the evidence?

See also: Schema, LLMs & The Low Bar For ‘Evidence’ In GEO

The findings reinforced the concern I raised in my earlier work about how easy-button AI content-generation systems create “me-too content parity.” As I explained to this brand, most of the GEO efforts to date were what I would call SEO-adjacent GEO. It is a newly rebranded SEO process that leverages AI speed for traditional content-gap analysis. Teams look at competitor coverage, People Also Ask questions, common prompts, and pages receiving citations, then create content to close those gaps. There is nothing inherently wrong with that. In this case, it appears to have worked extremely well at establishing this company’s category presence. The limitation appears when everyone says roughly the same thing, and you don’t offer details.

If every vendor claims to be end-to-end, highly integrated, AI-enabled, scalable, flexible, and focused on efficiency, those statements may help establish that each belongs in the category. They do much less when the model has to determine what “end-to-end” covers, what “integration” connects, what the AI actually does, or what evidence supports the efficiency claim.

Parity can help a company qualify for the conversation without providing enough information to survive deeper evaluation.

See also: AI Gives You The Vocabulary. It Doesn’t Give You The Expertise

Running the prompts was relatively straightforward. There wasn’t a tool or off-the-shelf framework for what we were trying to measure. We needed to build a multi-variable evidence scorecard capable of determining whether the model had enough support to evaluate the company against the criteria that actually mattered in the buying decision.

That meant defining what counted as strong evidence, what first-party documentation was enough, and when independent corroboration became important. The bigger challenge was thinking beyond the “we are great” organizational mindset to determine when an evidence gap or omission was serious enough to affect consideration or selection. That turned out to be even harder than getting SEOs to agree on ranking factors.

Those judgments then had to be translated into a repeatable scoring system and applied consistently across the prompts and five AI surfaces. It also required conversations beyond content. Sales had to help identify criteria and objections that affect deals. Product had to validate capabilities and limitations. Customer-facing teams helped identify real outcomes. In some cases, customers themselves were the best source for understanding what evidence mattered.

This is why I do not believe there will be one universal GEO evidence scorecard that every organization can download. The methodology can be reusable if you can honestly define the decision criteria and what constitutes sufficient evidence, then evaluate whether AI can connect the brand to those criteria.

As we evaluated eligibility criteria and evidence requirements, many findings no longer looked like conventional content gaps. Looking at this massive evidence gap matrix as one manager exclaimed, “that no human will ever read,” it was obvious this would become a team effort to make the changes. Content teams cannot make up content, nor will they necessarily know how everything works.

Some of the knowledge required to close these gaps is organizational in nature. We had to pry it out of product, engineering, and sales, and then run it through marketing and compliance. In some cases, we had to loop in external and partner relations when stronger third-party or attributable proof was required beyond what we could create.

The content team still has a critical role: organize that knowledge, make relationships explicit, create coherent evidence paths, and expose information in ways humans and AI systems can understand.

Most importantly, this project validated and reinforced my belief that measuring only the first AI response is insufficient. True, those broad PAA-like prompts are valuable for generating a list of options worth considering. The problem is the current tools cannot maintain state like a human workflow and aggregate the sequence of prompt turns most users leverage. Each subsequent prompt digs deeper into the criteria for consideration, maintaining preferences, and memory, and culminating in the multivariate result that I affectionately call prompt math.

This means that GEO measurement needs to become more sophisticated and account for both visibility and decision readiness. Many tools now have answer accuracy monitoring, which is a step forward. I argue that we need to put more effort into the element that is much closer to the business problem organizations should actually care about.

There is an obvious danger here because once marketers conclude that AI systems need evidence, we will probably see an explosion of “evidence-shaped content”: longer pages, superficial diagrams, weak surveys, synthetic research, and AI-generated case studies created primarily because someone believes those formats will influence an LLM, just like the explosion of Reddit submission services. That misses the point. The objective is not to manufacture artifacts that appear persuasive to AI, but to provide real content that reduces uncertainty and risk for the buyer.

If the information would not help a knowledgeable customer make a better decision, it probably should not exist merely to improve a GEO score.

As noted, the company had already achieved what many GEO programs would consider an amazing success: 98.4% visibility and 93.2% primary-or-shortlist consideration, yet 73.7% of those same responses had weak or missing evidence. Unfortunately, our analysis could not prove that this evidence gap caused every lost sale or every subsequent AI exclusion. What it does show is that a visibility-only measurement model would have missed the weakness we were asked to investigate.

Any company can succeed in entering the conversation but fail to offer enough credible evidence to remain qualified as the conversation becomes more specific.

Reviving an Enduring Emblem of Simplicity, Loll Designs Launches the Here and There Collection

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

The best designs almost always come out of necessity and quiet observation. In the 1940s, legendary ecologist Aldo Leopold constructed a remarkably straightforward wooden bench so that he could sit outside his Wisconsin farmstead and contemplate nature’s primal force; the fundamental need for its conservation. The back-slanted seat was constructed from readily available dimensional lumber and almost immediately presented itself as an act of unpretentious, serendipitous genius: the quick assembly of a functional furnishing devised for a specific need but ultimately, one with universal potential. The particular brilliance of the design was its x-shaped self-bracing geometry, zero waste configuration, and uncomplicated assembly.

“Anyone that’s handy with a saw and some fasteners and lumber could easily build an Aldo bench,” says Eric Swanson, president of Duluth-based outdoor furniture brand Loll Designs. “That’s the beauty of it.” The Minnesota manufacturer recently teamed up with Michigan industrial design studio Argenta Park to turn the foundational artifact into the Here and There Collection. Named for Leopold’s belief that our appreciation for the natural world should balance between what’s nearby and farther afield, the cohesive and complementary suite of furnishings includes a dining chair, dining table, and of course, a bench.

This contemporary reinterpretation was twelve years in the making. For his outdoor wedding in 2014, Argenta Park designer Ricky Biddle was faced with the same practical challenge Leopold surmounted decades earlier: creating sturdy, durable, and comfortable outdoor seating. He didn’t want to rent generic, flimsy event furniture, so he spent his bachelor party assembling rudimentary Aldo benches. It was almost as if influential Italian designer Enzo Mari had sanctioned the task as one of his open-source “Autoprogettazione” self designs.

The pieces have remained a fixture of Biddle and his family’s everyday life ever since. This permanence prompted him to reconsider how its raw genius might be retooled using advanced manufacturing, elevated ergonomics, and year-round durability. He brought the idea to Argenta Park founder Churu Yun and the Loll Designs engineering team.

The best type of historical preservation is the kind that implements the latest technologies and refined strategies; doesn’t focus on creating replicas merely out of nostalgic reverence. “Not all historical designs lend themselves to being evolved into a modern-day furniture piece,” says Brian King, Loll Designs Brand and Marketing Director. “It’s more about being hyper selective in a design that has meaning, history, or a deep-rooted memory and identifying what’s most valuable.” Loll had done this many times before, most notably by bringing the ubiquitous Adirondack Chair up to contemporary standards.

While still carrying the original design’s understated silhouette, the re-edited and expanded collection carefully addressed the physical limitations of simple timber construction. Softly contoured surfaces replace flat planks, streamlined fastener assembly, and the use of 100% recyclable high-density polyethylene (HDPE)—available in Black, Charcoal Grey, Fog, and Sage—simplifies ongoing maintenance.

This calculus responds to changing demands: contemporary homeowners are increasingly looking for low-maintenance solutions that can withstand harsh seasonal shifts without sacrificing aesthetic refinement or tactile comfort. “With historical designs comes historical maintenance and poor ergonomics,” Swanson adds. “People want to enjoy their time outdoors sitting in their furniture more than working on it, which is why using alternative materials to traditional wood is a benefit; why a curved seat and backrest is so important; and why the right proportions—the height of the dining table—make all the difference.”

Leopold is widely considered the founder of wildlife ecology and his bench stands testament to that; allowing people to enjoy nature for as long as they want. The new Here and There Collection carries on that legacy.

Walnut, leather, stone, and brass shape a new collection for the home, debuting at High Point Market this fall

Barker Associates Architecture Office pairs preserved architectural details with saturated hues, custom woodwork, and contemporary art in a four-story Clinton Hill home for empty nesters

JetBlue unveils new first-class seats to bring back profits

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

JetBlue Airways unveiled its new domestic first-class seats Wednesday as more of the country's carriers bet on the premium travel boom to make them profitable again.

JetBlue announced it would offer a domestic first class — BlueFirst — in December 2024 and plans to start flying aircraft with the new seats and full on-board product next month. The carrier showed off the new cabin at its home hub at John F. Kennedy International Airport on Wednesday. Retrofits will continue during 2027, CEO Joanna Geraghty told CNBC late last month.

JetBlue will have 12 of the seats on its Airbus A320 fleet and 30 extra-legroom seats, down from 42 of those options previously. Because the airplanes don't have ovens, JetBlue said it is offering an array of cold food options like sesame noodles, pastrami sandwiches and tortellini salad, though they vary by route.

The rollout comes as the New York airline tries to stabilize after last posting a profit in 2019. Last month, JetBlue executives sought to calm investors after shares in the airline hit a record low following a report that bondholders had contracted restructuring advisors.

"We have no intention, no plans to file for bankruptcy. We are not engaged with any restructuring company," Geraghty told CNBC in an interview on Oct. 27.

Geraghty told CNBC's Phil LeBeau on Wednesday that JetBlue has "met with a few of the creditors over the last couple of weeks as part of ... normal investor engagement." She said the company made it clear to bondholders that JetBlue doesn't need to reorganize.

The airline's shares are up more than 40% this month, more than other passenger carriers, as of midday Wednesday, though they are still off about 11% so far this year.

JetBlue is betting that resilient travel demand, especially for roomier seats will help fuel its turnaround plan, which aims to get the carrier to an operating profit next year and $1 in adjusted earnings per share in 2028.

Airline executives have said they expect fares to stay elevated next year as they grapple with higher costs, including but not limited to fuel, such as labor and airport fees. Geraghty said Wednesday that even if fuel prices moderate next year, "I don't think fares are going to fall as much as people expect."

Airlines large and small are racing to outfit their cabins with roomier, more expensive seating options to help cover ever-higher costs, from jet fuel to labor to airport operation fees.

For example, a ticket in the new recliner first-class seats between Kennedy airport and Fort Lauderdale-Hollywood International Airport in Florida is going for $847 roundtrip in the first week of March while standard economy is $446.

On Tuesday, budget carrier Frontier Airlines unveiled its new first-class seats, which will take up the first two rows, and said it would outfit its Airbus fleet with premium seating that has extra legroom and in-seat power through row eight. The airline will take out a row of seating on its planes to make the pricier ones fit, Chief Marketing Officer Bobby Schroeter told CNBC in an interview Monday.

JetBlue last month reported that it ended the third quarter with $2.2 billion in liquidity and that its unencumbered asset base is now $7 billion, including $2.8 billion in aircraft and engines.

JetBlue had total debt of $8.4 billion as of Sept. 30, the carrier said in its earnings filing.

Geraghty told CNBC last month there is a "fundamental disconnect between the narrative in the bond market and what our financial results actually demonstrate."

When asked whether JetBlue's eye on keeping costs down includes furloughs, layoffs or buyouts, Geraghty last month said "absolutely not."
