This magic mushroom can kill

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Every print subscription comes with full digital access

Hallucinogens and poisons have never been found in the same mushroom — until now

Meet the dark magic mushroom, Galerina indica. The tiny fungus makes both a hallucinogen and a deadly toxin.

This is a human-written story voiced by AI. Got feedback? Take our survey . (See our AI policy here .)

People who eat this tiny magic mushroom may hit a lethal low instead of getting high.

The fungus makes hallucinogenic psilocybin and deadly amatoxin, researchers report November 11 in Current Biology. Dubbed “dark magic mushrooms,” it is the first species of fungus discovered to make both types of compounds.

Chemist and fungal biologist Andrew Kunik found the dark magic mushroom in 2025 growing on a decaying moss-covered palm trunk in a central Florida wetland. Kunik, who was then doing post-baccalaureate research in the lab of fungal biologist Matthew Smith at the University of Florida in Gainesville, identified the mini mushroom as Galerina indica.

We summarize the week's scientific breakthroughs every Thursday.

That species had not been reported in Florida before, Kunik says. “It’s quite rare and unusual.” He also found two other new related species, tagged with the placeholder names Galerina species 1 and Galerina species 2.

Other researchers had previously described G. indica’s appearance and found DNA signatures that distinguish the fungus from related species. But no one had cataloged the mushroom’s complete chemical or genetic makeup.

Related Galerina species make amatoxins, deadly chemicals that can kill in even tiny amounts and are found in death cap mushrooms and other species. Kunik and colleagues looked for those toxins in G. indica and the two other new Galerina species. G. indica makes a previously undiscovered form of amatoxin the team is calling alpha-proamanitin, the chemical analysis revealed.

The small mushroom also had blue bruises, a clue that it might make psilocybin, a psychedelic chemical being tested as a treatment for mental health disorders. Fungi usually make either amatoxin or psilocybin. Not both. Galerina species 1 also makes psilocybin but doesn’t make amatoxin, and species 2 makes neither.

“This finding shows that there’s a lot more diversity in [fungal] chemistry and the ways that they’re combined than we had really thought about,” says Anne Pringle, a mycologist at the University of Wisconsin–Madison who was not involved in the work.

Discovering these dual compounds in one mushroom was both exciting and required further analysis to confirm, Kunik says. “Surprising discoveries require great depth of evidence,” he says.

A complete workup of the mushroom’s DNA revealed genes for producing both types of chemicals. Ancestors of these dark magic mushrooms probably picked up those genes by swapping DNA with other species. Such DNA swapping, called horizontal gene transfer, is one way that organisms can quickly evolve new traits, like when bacteria exchange antibiotic resistance genes with unrelated species.

Ancestors of G. indica and its relatives probably acquired amatoxin genes early on. Those toxins are made by many of G. indica’s cousins, which indicates that amatoxins are probably heirlooms passed down from common ancestors. Ancestors of species 1 and species 2 seem to have dropped the amatoxin genes at some point since they no longer make the compounds.

The two psilocybin-producing species may have independently obtained the psychedelic genes in separate DNA swap meets, or a common ancestor may have picked up the genes and then passed them down to G. indica and species 1. There’s not enough known about the fungi’s family tree to sort out which scenario is correct.

And there’s another unsolved evolutionary mystery, Pringle says. “We still don’t know why mushrooms are poisonous.” Many scientists think it is for defending against predators, but hard evidence is lacking, she says.

“Can I eat it?” is often the first question people ask when finding a new mushroom, Pringle says. “I’m always so uncomfortable with that question and that conversation. And this paper confirms that, in fact, you should be uncomfortable.” After all, eating these dark magic mushrooms could send people tripping into the grave.

eToro Q3 Profit Drops 74% as Crypto Trading Activity Slows

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: eToro reported third-quarter net income of about $15 million, down from $57 million a year earlier, as crypto-related activity weakened. Its funded-account base continued to grow, highlighting the difference between attracting customers and generating trading revenue from them in any particular quarter.

A trading platform can gain users even when the market becomes less profitable to trade. That tension runs through eToro’s latest financial results, which showed weaker quarterly earnings despite an expanding customer base.

According to the company’s November 10 earnings release, third-quarter net income was approximately $15 million, a decline of about 74% from $57 million in the comparable period. Net contribution came to around $186 million, down 13% year over year, with lower cryptocurrency trading activity contributing to the drop.

At the same time, eToro reported 4.39 million funded accounts, an increase of 18%, and assets under administration of approximately $20.8 billion. It also announced a $250 million share repurchase authorization.

Brokerage and trading businesses earn revenue from a mix of activity, product pricing and other services. When customers trade less frequently, a growing account base may not immediately offset the effect. The relationship is particularly visible in crypto, where transaction volumes can shift quickly as market sentiment changes.

That is why the funded-account figure and the net-contribution figure should be assessed together. One shows a measure of customer acquisition and retention; the other gives more insight into the contribution earned from services in the quarter.

Assets under administration are also distinct from eToro’s own cash and corporate assets. They reflect client-related asset values that can be affected by price movements as well as client activity.

Lower crypto activity at one trading platform does not prove digital assets are in a long-term decline. Users may trade through different venues, switch into other asset classes or simply become less active during a choppy period. The business may also earn different amounts from different types of orders.

Still, eToro’s numbers offer an operating-business perspective on the volatility seen in public-market crypto demand. NewsBTC has been tracking recent Bitcoin ETF outflows as well as uneven demand for spot Ether funds. ETF subscriptions and brokerage transaction revenues are separate metrics, but both help describe how investors are participating in the market.

A fall in net contribution can also reflect conditions in noncrypto products. Readers should avoid attributing every change in a diversified platform’s results to Bitcoin prices alone.

The $250 million repurchase authorization gives eToro permission to buy back shares under its program, subject to the company’s terms. It is not proof that the full amount was repurchased on November 10, nor does it guarantee future share-price gains.

Repurchases can alter the share count and capital allocation over time, but their effect depends on execution price, cash available and how the company balances other investments. They are best evaluated alongside earnings quality and strategic spending.

Investors should watch whether funded-account growth leads to sustained activity and improved net contribution in subsequent quarters. The product mix matters: strength in equities or other services could help offset a quieter crypto market, while increased marketing and operating costs could limit the benefit.

For crypto observers, the report is a reminder that adoption and profitability do not always move together. eToro gained funded accounts, but the softer trading environment made it harder to translate that scale into quarterly profit.

3 Winterization Projects That Are as Easy as Painting

📅 2026-11-11  |  Read Source →

100+ Most Unique and Practical Gifts 🎁 for Homeowners and DIYers

Published Nov 11, 2026 10:30 AM EST

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn More ›

Tips, tricks & ideas for a better home and yard, delivered to your inbox daily.

Winter weather can be devastating to outdoor furniture, appliances, and structures, which is why many people store as much as possible in a covered shed or garage throughout the cold season. Even though not everything can be neatly tucked away, such as your deck, walkways, and gates, you can take steps to prepare them for harsh winter conditions before snow starts to fall.

Outdoor winterization projects that involve painting, staining, or sealing help surfaces create a durable barrier against freeze-thaw cycles, moisture intrusion, and corrosive deicing salts. It’s important to complete these protective jobs before the first hard freeze so you can prevent deep structural damage during the winter months.

Leaving wood decks, fences, or patio furniture bare during winter allows moisture from snow and ice to seep into the wood pores. When trapped water freezes, it expands, causing the wood to split, warp, and rot. A fresh coat of waterproof sealant or oil-based stain acts as a hydrophobic barrier. Water pools on top of the sealed surface rather than penetrating the wood grain.

Before applying a compatible exterior stain or water-repellent finish, clean the surface, remove failing finish, and allow the wood to dry sufficiently. For decking and steps, choose a winterization product specifically approved for horizontal surfaces and foot traffic.

Marc Sharkey, owner of Dynamic Painting Inc, says that “the finish reduces water absorption from rain and melting snow. Repeated wetting and drying can contribute to checking, splitting, and warping; prolonged dampness also creates conditions for decay.

“Pay particular attention to exposed board ends and areas where the old finish has worn away.” He also warns: “A new coating cannot repair rotten wood.” Given that Sharkey’s business is based in Alberta, he has experience dealing with some of the harshest winters in North America.

This oil-based formula provides corrosion resistance and weather protection for outdoor metal surfaces . Learn more in our buyer’s guide.

Winter preparation is important to safeguard your home. Chipped paint on steel or iron railings, furniture, and gates exposes the raw metal to oxygen and winter humidity, rapidly accelerating the oxidation process. Road salt or salt-based sidewalk ice melts further expedite this corrosion, eating through the metal structures. Applying a coat of exterior, rust-resistant enamel or oil-based paint seals out moisture and oxygen, halting the chemical reaction that creates rust.

It’s a budget-friendly winterization project that also forms a slick, chemical-resistant barrier against salt splash-back. Before applying a coating, remove loose rust and flaking paint and clean away dirt and salt. Then apply an appropriate rust-inhibiting primer and exterior metal finish. Check compatibility carefully: steel, aluminum, and galvanized metal can require different preparation and primers.

“An intact coating limits exposure to moisture and oxygen, helping slow corrosion. Left exposed, rust can spread beneath surrounding paint, causing more peeling and pitting. Over time, severe corrosion can weaken components. Painting over loose rust does not solve the underlying problem,” explains Sharkey.

Moisture from ice and snow can cause significant damage to concrete because it is a highly porous material. When snow melts on walkways, driveways, or patios, the concrete absorbs the water. Subsequent freezing temperatures cause that internal water to expand, which leads to surface flaking, scaling, and deep cracking.

Unlike surface coatings, penetrating sealers chemically bond inside concrete pores. They create an invisible, breathable hydrophobic barrier that prevents liquid water from sinking into the stone, while still allowing internal vapor to escape.

Sharkey notes that “for compatible, absorbent concrete steps, walkways, or patios, a penetrating sealer rated for exterior concrete and freeze-thaw exposure can reduce water absorption. Confirm compatibility with any existing treatment and follow the preparation instructions.”

Reducing water and deicing-salt penetration helps protect against winter deterioration. However, just keep in mind that a sealer is preventive maintenance; it does not repair cracks, failing concrete, or drainage problems.
